--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 1:**
- Champion (17264): 2-state FSM with Repulsion (state 0) + Spiral (state 1). State 0 repels with probability transition; State 1 spirals and transitions on seeing green (color 2) or gray floor.
- Score gap: Champion at 17264, 2nd at 18066, 3rd at 18802. The champion is significantly better.
- The mission is AGGREGATION - robots need to get to the BLUE-walled strip (BLACK floor). So robots should move toward the black floor region.

**Key insight:** The target region has BLACK floor and BLUE walls. Robots should:
1. Move toward blue walls (GoToColor with blue=3) or away from green walls (GoAwayColor green=2)
2. Once on black floor, stay there or aggregate

**Champion analysis:** Repulsion + Spiral combination works well. The spiral explores, and on seeing green (color 2 = green walls) it transitions. This makes sense - seeing green means you're near the wrong end, so spiral away.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exactly (elitism)
2. Exploit: tweak champion parameters (probabilities, gains, speeds)
3. Explore: try GoToColor blue (clr=3), GoAwayColor green (clr=2), floor-color-based transitions to black floor

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion (exact copy)**
2. **Exploit**: Same topology, adjust rep gain and probabilities
3. **Exploit**: Same topology, different spiral speed
4. **Exploit**: Add floor-black condition to spiral state
5. **Exploit**: Swap to GoAwayColor green + Spiral
6. **Explore**: 3-state: Exploration → GoToColor blue → Stop on black
7. **Explore**: 2-state: GoToColor blue + Repulsion
8. **Explore**: 3-state: Repulsion + GoToColor blue + Exploration
9. **Explore**: 2-state: GoAwayColor green + Spiral
10. **Explore**: 3-state with floor sensing
11. **Explore**: Repulsion + GoToColor blue
12. **Explore**: Spiral + GoToColor blue
13. **Explore**: FlockCenter + GoToColor blue
14. **Explore**: 3-state comprehensive

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 2:**
- New champion (4363): Repulsion(state 0) → GoToColor blue(state 1). State 0 transitions to GoToColor blue when seeing blue (color 3) OR with prob 0.4. State 1 (GoToColor blue) transitions back to Repulsion when on black floor (p=0.8) OR with prob 0.02.
- 2nd place (4725): GoAwayColor green(state 0) → Spiral(state 1). GoAway green transitions to Spiral on black floor or prob 0.05. Spiral transitions back on seeing green or gray floor.
- 3rd place (5294): 3-state with Repulsion+Spiral+GoToColor blue.

**Key insights:**
- The champion works by: repel (spreading out) until seeing blue walls or randomly → GoToColor blue → once on black floor, go back to repulsion (staying put since black floor = target).
- Wait - if on black floor, going back to repulsion might push them away. Let me re-read: transition from GoToColor blue back to Repulsion when on BLACK floor (p=0.8). But then repulsion would push them around the target area. The black floor detection is the key trigger.
- Actually, the transition from state 1 (GoToColor) to state 0 (Repulsion) when on black floor means: once arrived, switch to repulsion (which keeps them from clustering too tight but they stay in the area since they keep detecting blue and going back).

**Strategy for Round 3:**
- Keep champion exactly
- Exploit champion: vary rep gain (2.5-4.0), probability values, color detection
- Exploit 2nd place: GoAwayColor green + Spiral with tweaks
- Try: make GoToColor blue the primary state with repulsion secondary
- Try: add a 3rd state for staying on black floor
- Try: reduce the 0.4 probability in state 0 (maybe too high, causes premature transitions)
- Try: adding neighbor count conditions

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 3:**
- Champion (4301): Exploration(rwm=15) → GoToColor blue(3). State 0 transitions on seeing blue(p=0.6) or prob 0.3. State 1 (GoToColor blue) transitions back on black floor(p=0.8) or prob 0.02.
- 2nd (4317): Repulsion + GoToColor blue with neighbor-below condition added.
- 3rd (4389): Same topology as champion but with Repulsion instead of Exploration, slightly different params.
- 4th (4475): Champion from round 2 (Repulsion+GoToColor).
- Score gap is small at top (4301-4580), then bigger jump to 4856+.

**Key pattern:** State 0 = exploration/repulsion (spreading), transitions to GoToColor blue when seeing blue walls or randomly. State 1 = GoToColor blue, transitions back to state 0 when on black floor (arrived!) or very small random prob.

**The logic:** Robots explore/repel, when they spot blue walls they navigate toward them. Once on black floor (target), they briefly switch back to exploration/repulsion to spread out in the target zone, then quickly get back to GoToColor blue. The very low p=0.02 for random transition from state 1 means they mostly stay in GoToColor blue until reaching black floor.

**Exploitation ideas:**
- Vary rwm (turning rate): try 10, 20, 25 for exploration
- Vary probability of seeing blue trigger: 0.5-0.7
- Vary random transition prob: 0.2-0.4
- Vary p_black floor in state 1: 0.7-0.9
- Try adding gray floor condition in state 0 to transition
- Try Exploration with different rwm as state 0

**Exploration ideas:**
- Try 3-state with exploration → GoToColor blue → Stop on black
- Try swapping: GoToColor blue first, then exploration if stuck
- Try adding neighbor count conditions

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**
- Champion (4132): Exploration(rwm=12) with 3 transitions: see blue(p=0.6), random(p=0.28), gray floor(p=0.5) → all go to GoToColor blue. State 1 (GoToColor blue): black floor(p=0.8) or random(p=0.02) → back to exploration.
- The top 9 machines are very tightly clustered (4132-4317), all using the same 2-state topology.
- 3-state machines did badly (ranks 13-14), confirming 2-state is better here.
- Key finding: Adding gray floor transition (c=1, p=0.5) in state 0 helped (champion). This makes sense: gray = middle of arena, not target, so transition to GoToColor blue.

**What varies among top performers:**
- rwm: 10-20, best seems 12-15
- p_blue_seen: 0.55-0.65, best ~0.6
- p_random: 0.25-0.35, best ~0.28-0.3
- p_gray (champion only): 0.5
- p_black in state 1: 0.75-0.9, best ~0.8-0.9
- p_random_s1: 0.01-0.02

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Keep champion
- Vary rwm: 8, 10, 12, 14
- Vary p_gray: 0.4, 0.45, 0.55, 0.6
- Vary p_blue: 0.55-0.65
- Vary p_random in s0: 0.2-0.35
- Try adding black floor condition in s0 (to NOT transition when already on black)
- Try adding white floor condition

**Exploration:**
- Try replacing gray condition with neighbor-below (if isolated, seek blue)
- Try different rwm values more aggressively

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 5:**
- All top 13 machines are tightly clustered (4109-4291), confirming the 2-state topology is highly stable.
- Champion: Exploration(rwm=12) with 3 transitions: see blue(0.6), random(0.28), neighbors-below-2 with steep sigmoid(w=8) → GoToColor blue. GoToColor blue: black floor(0.8) or random(0.02) → back.
- The neighbor-below condition (c=4, p=2, w=8) means: if fewer than 2 neighbors nearby, transition to GoToColor blue. This is smart - isolated robots navigate toward target.
- 2nd place: 4-transition state 0 adding black floor detection too.

**Key observations:**
- rwm=12 seems slightly best but 8-15 all work well
- p_blue=0.6 is consistently good
- The neighbor condition (c=4) with threshold=2, steepness=8 is new and effective
- ZigZag fails badly as state 0 replacement
- The scores are converging - need more aggressive exploration

**Exploitation:**
- Vary w (steepness) of neighbor condition: 5, 10, 12, 15
- Vary neighbor threshold: 1, 3
- Combine both gray floor AND neighbor-below conditions
- Vary p_random in s0: 0.22-0.32
- Try p_black in s1: 0.75-0.85

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**
- Champion unchanged at 4109.19 (from round 5)
- All 13 machines this round scored 4131-4309, very tight band
- The topology is completely locked in: 2-state, Exploration(rwm≈12-15) → GoToColor blue, with 3 transitions from state 0
- Scores are not improving significantly - we're in a local optimum

**What we've learned:**
- rwm: 12-15 seems best (15 was rank 1 this round with 4131)
- Neighbor-below threshold: p=2 seems best (p=1 and p=3 are slightly worse)
- Steepness w: 5-12 all work similarly (8 seems fine)
- p_random in s0: 0.22-0.28 both similar
- Adding 4th transition (gray floor or black floor) slightly hurts
- Adding 3rd transition to state 1 hurts

**The system seems stuck near 4100-4150. Need genuine exploration to escape.**

**Exploration strategies:**
1. Try replacing GoToColor blue in state 1 with Stop (once on black floor, stop completely)
2. Try much lower rwm (5) or higher (25, 30)
3. Try GoAwayColor green as state 0 instead of exploration
4. Try neighbor-above in state 0 (if many neighbors, go to target)
5. Try changing p_black in state 1 to 0.9 or 0.95
6. Try 3-state: Explore → GoToColor blue → Stop on black
7. Try different color for camera (magenta=5, cyan=6)
8. Try FlockCenter as state 0

**Key insight I haven't tried:** What if the transition from GoToColor blue back to Exploration happens ONLY on black floor with much higher prob (0.95), making robots stay in target longer before re-exploring?

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 7 - Major breakthrough!**
- New champion (3020): FlockCenter(fc=50) → GoToColor blue! Score jumped from ~4109 to 3020 - a 26% improvement!
- 2nd place (3277): GoAwayColor green → GoToColor blue - also very good!
- Both of these replaced Exploration with a more directed behavior in state 0

**Why FlockCenter works so well:** Robots cluster together first, then when they spot blue walls (or randomly), they all navigate to blue together as a group. The neighbor-below condition (isolated robots → seek blue) is also key.

**Why GoAwayColor green works:** Moving away from green walls pushes robots toward the blue end naturally.

**Key pattern preserved in both top performers:**
- State 1: GoToColor blue(3), transitions: black floor(p=0.8) or random(p=0.02)
- State 0: 3 transitions - see blue(p=0.6), random(p=0.28), neighbor-below-2 (w=8)

**Exploitation of champion (FlockCenter + GoToColor blue):**
- Vary fc: 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80
- Vary p_blue_seen: 0.5-0.7
- Vary p_random: 0.2-0.35
- Vary neighbor threshold and steepness
- Try fc=50 with different s1 params

**Exploitation of 2nd place (GoAwayColor green + GoToColor blue):**
- Vary probabilities

**Exploration:**
- Try Attraction + GoToColor blue (robots group then navigate)
- Try FlockCenter with GoAwayColor green as state 1
- Try 3-state: FlockCenter → GoToColor blue → Stop

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 8:**
- Champion unchanged at 3020.93 (FlockCenter fc=50)
- Round 8 best was 3254 (GoAwayColor green), 2nd was 3412 (Attraction att=3)
- Both GoAwayColor green and Attraction work well as state 0 alternatives
- FlockCenter fc=50 scored 3663 this round (variance in simulation)
- fc=40 (3575) beats fc=65 (3682) beats fc=80 (3925) - lower fc seems better
- 3-state machines fail badly again

**Key observations:**
- GoAwayColor green (clr0=2) with black floor transition (p=0.8) and random (p=0.05) → GoToColor blue is strong
- Attraction (att=3) with same 3-transition pattern is also strong
- Champion's specific params: fc=50, p_blue=0.6, p_rand=0.28, neighbor-below-2 w=8
- The "see blue + random + neighbor-below" trigger pattern for state 0 seems robust

**What to try in Round 9:**
1. Keep champion
2. Exploit GoAwayColor green variant more (vary p_black, p_rand)
3. Exploit Attraction variant (vary att gain 2.0-4.0)
4. Try combining GoAwayColor green with neighbor-below condition
5. Try FlockCenter with lower fc (20, 30)
6. Try fc=50 with different p_blue and p_rand
7. Try Attraction with neighbor-below condition
8. Try GoAwayColor green → GoToColor blue with 4 transitions
9. Try fc=35

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 9:**
- Champion still at 3020.93 (FlockCenter fc=50)
- Round 9 top performers: Attraction att=3 (3271), Attraction att=4 (3299), GoAwayColor green (3337), FlockCenter fc=50 (3344)
- Attraction and FlockCenter both cluster robots before directing them to blue - this is the winning strategy
- fc=30 and fc=35 perform worse (3958, 4108) - too little flocking force sends robots to corners
- fc=50 is the sweet spot for FlockCenter
- att=3 and att=4 both score ~3271-3299, att=2 is slightly worse (3435)

**Key patterns confirmed:**
- The 3-transition state 0 pattern (see blue + random + neighbor-below-2 w=8) is robust
- GoToColor blue as state 1 with p_black=0.8, p_rand=0.02 is robust
- State 0 behavior options ranked: FlockCenter(50) > Attraction(3-4) > GoAwayColor(green) > Exploration

**This is the final round - maximize exploitation of the best found patterns:**
1. Champion (exact copy)
2. Try Attraction att=3.5 (between 3 and 4)
3. Try Attraction att=5 (push limit)
4. Try FlockCenter fc=45, 55 (near 50)

9. Try att=3 with p_rand=0.25 in s0

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
